‘Telephone: SS w maa -

At aprox 01.00 on the morning of Sunday 22 June, Section 40) | and his
wife were driving their Land Rover north from Ulverston to Silloth,

using the unclassified road over Corney Fell.

Ahead of them, at a height estimated at 1000 feet, they saw three
extremely bright lights, one to the left, one to the right and one
in front, forming a huge triangle. The lights were of similar
brightness to a welding torch and were flickering in an irregular
manner. jSection 40) described them as being identical to those he saw
on a video Or unexplained sightings in Scotland seen on a TV
documentary on Monday or Tuesday this week.

: formerly in the army but probably now in retirement,
hesitated to make this report lest he be considered a crank, but in
view of the proximity of Sellafield (aprox 10 miles NW) he decided
to ring and say what he had seen in case o£ security implications.

can verify the report. Over to you!

that he had received a "UFO" report from a member of the public.
The sighting occurred at 02:36 on 16 June and was described as
solid silver objects seen below cloud level. If the MOD required
any further info, we should phone Section 40| He did not give
the informant's name or area in which the "UFO" was seen.

WEEK BEGINNING 26 MAY 1997

26/05/97

Answerphone switched off due to the Spring Bank Holiday.
27/05/97

No messages

28/05/97

29/05/97

No messages

30/05/97

At 09:11, Section 40) | telephoned to report an unusual sight in the
sky above her home, which is in Suffolk. At 11:55pm on 29/05/97,
she saw 10 - 20 objects that were very far off. She looked through
binoculars and saw that the objects were flashing red, orange and
reen like traffic lights. The objects were stationary and silent.
her husband and her daughter (aged 27) saw the lights
until 1:30am. She phoned RAF Mildenhall and they told her they had
no military aircraft in the sky that could account for the
sighting, and there were no exercises going on that they were
aware of. Section 40| | also contacted Ipswich Police who said that no
other "UFO" reports had been made. She wondered if we could tell

"UNIDENTIFIED" AIRCRAFT REPORT BY AER LINGUS CREW -— 18 APR 97

1. As agreed in recent exchanges of correspondence concerning
"UFO" policy, Sec(AS) is under remit to forward any "unidentified"
aerial activity reports received by credible witnesses to you.

2.0 Attached please find a copy of a report (which we only
received this week) of an unidentified aircraft made by the crew
of an Aer Lingus flight from Dublin to Amsterdam on Friday 18
April. On the face of it this would appear to be a flight safety
issue and one on which the CAA would have the lead.

3. As yet we have received no enquiries from members of the

public about this matter, but should there be anything in this of
particular interest to either of you then I should be grateful if
you would keep Sec(AS) in the picture.

Sec(AS)2al

15/04/97

sqn idx SEER from Boscombe pown BERIBMED phoned to say he

had received a report from a BEM. He gave brief details. I
phoned for a fuller picture and ome
address. She saw a saucer shaped object over the trees-in her back
garden at 2145 on 11 April. It was silent, hovering, it had red,
green and blue lights on it and her four children saw it too. Her

eit ON Ferndown, Dorset. Tel:

16/04/97

Sqn ey Scion 4 CRO Honnington, rang and spoke to KP. In
accorda standing instructions a Junior Technician
reported to him that at 21104 on Tue 11 March he saw something he
thought he ought to report.

A mile to two from Honnington he saw a light fly across the sky
like a flashing camera 200 ft off the ground and it was followed

30 seconds later by a low flying jet.
Discussion with the CRO concluded it was most likely two aircraft

and he did not see the first aircraft clearly. No concerns raised
by CRO, just thought it ought to be highlighted for the record.

EES from Lowestoft, suffolk. TE ZUME phoned at

12:33. At 9:15 on 9 April she saw a bright white light that came
from the south. It was very quick, and was a circle of lights that
had a triangle in the middle. It had three big mauve/blue spots in
the triangle. It hovered, moved across the sky the shot off to the
west. She reported it to RAF Coltishall who said that over the
last two weeks they had received several reports.

Section 40__ AR Nemrrs at 11:16 to report a sighting. On 31/03/97 at
10pm she and her partner, saw lights in the
sky which were white then turned green, red and blue. They stayed
in one position for a while then moved from left to right. In an
hour they had moved across the sky. There were planes in the sky
at the time but she didn't think it was a plane or a helicopter as
it hovered _in the same place for so long. Her address is: 40|

Didcot, Oxford. Tel: ee

ACTION: Report held on file 64/2 part E enc 70

telephoned at 11:56 to report a sighting on
behalf of her daughter. At 11:45pm on 31/03/97, she saw an object
in the sky with pieces of fire coming from it. It was heading
towards two petrol stations and she and the 12 other witnesses
thought it was going to crash into the garages. It wasn't the
comet as she could see that in the sky at the same time. She
thought it might have been a military aircraft in some sort of

trouble. Her address _is: Hemel Hempstead,
Hertfordshire. Tel:

ACTION: Wrote to SEeteeON informing her that there were no
military aircraft accidents or incidents that could have
accounted for the sighting. See file 64/3 part I enc 33
for reply and file 64/2 part E enc 71/1 for copy of
"UFO" report.

A switchboard operator telephoned at approximately 11:00am and
said that she had on the line. She felt that she
couldn't put him onto the answerphone as he is an educated person
and he wanted to speak to someone, not leave a message. I asked
her to put him through to the answerphone and no other number as
we do not make exceptions for anyone. At approximately 2pm, the
same switchboard operator phoned and said that Sea was
on the line again. He told the operator that he had left his

details on the answerphone and no-one had phoned him back yet. He
had not left any message at all on the answerphone.

work at 4am and saw a large lit up blue triangle shaped craft over
his back garden. It was silent, was about 200ft in length and was
lit up at each corner. Dogs in the area were barking which he said
was unusual. The object was hovering about 300-400ft in the air,
then it shot off and disappeared. It left a silky white substance

on the tree tops, and he has got some of this home.
The total sighting lasted for about 3 minutes. lives in
—— Birmingham and his telephone number is

ACTION: Report held on file 64/2 pt E enc 60
NFA required.

21/03/97

A female phoned at 1:44 to report a sighting seen above Newmarket.
She said she would phone back later to give more details like the
time and her address.

WEEK BEGINNING 10 MARCH 1997

10/03/97

Section 40,0 from Edmonton phoned to report a sighting he

observed on 5 March at 10:35pm. The object he saw was pink/red and
was moving in a north-westerly direction over Enfield/Bushell
Park. He remembers seeing a newspaper article 20 months ago with a
picture of a "UFO" that was the same shape as the one he spotted
on 5 March.

Section 40] Fama Gloucester (tel: Bedion ai) telephoned at 1716 to
report a sighting which she described as 5 round white glowing

objects observed at 9 o'clock (pm?) on Wed 26 Feb. They were
moving up & down and side to side and were quite fast. She wanted
to know whether anyone else had seen what she had observed.

ACTION: Report held on file 64/2 pt E Enc 46
NFA required.

28/02/97

(tel: SEO telephoned at 1145 to thank

for the letter she sent him (see file 64/3 part H enc 66).
He asked if she took appointments either in work or in her own
time and, if so, to phone him. If not, then she should disregard
the call.

ACTION: NFA required.

1.  Tamwriting, on behalf of the Station Commander, to acknowledge your telephone call of
31 January 1997 regarding the Unidentified Flying Objects that you saw in the vicinity of the
Rowntrees Factory at York, Sightings such as yours are dealt with by a department within the Air
Traffic Control Centre at West Drayton, which has been set up to investigate incidents such as you
described. The information is then passed on to the Ministry of Defence, so you may well receive a

reply in the near future.
2. Unfortunately, I.am unable to give you any information at this time, but I thank you for your
concern and for reporting the sighting.
Yours sincerely
Wing Commander
for Officer Commanding
